### ✅ 26 Oct 2026: THE MONTH -> WATERFALL SWITCH LAG FROM `71a6c87`, FIXED. Committed and pushed on the owner's picker (*"Commit"*, *"Commit and push"*)

- **Asked** (owner, after `71a6c87` went live): *"theres now a slight lag when you switch from the month view to the
  waterfall view"*.
- **Cause, measured** (visible browser pane, `mvnotes`, 1440×900, the click's synchronous time, 5 runs each): the
  build before `71a6c87` ~110 ms, `71a6c87` ~260 ms. Deleting the new rules live one at a time pinned it on ONE:
  `body:has(#view-month-btn.active) .preview-panel > *{flex:none}`. A `:has()` rule with a universal subject makes
  Chrome restyle far more when the view button's class flips. The other new `:has()` rules cost nothing measurable.
- **Fix** (`legacy.css` only): that rule deleted, with a ⛔ comment saying why. It was not needed, because a flex item
  never shrinks below its content (`min-height:auto`). After: month -> waterfall ~110 ms at 100% and from a 150% month,
  the same as before `71a6c87`.
- ⚠️ **Lesson:** never put `*` (or another broad subject) after a `body:has(...)` selector. Name the elements.
  ⚠️ **Measuring trap:** the browser pane, hidden, gives wild timings (0.1–3.5 s on the same build) and
  throttles rAF to ~1 fps. Show the pane, and time the click synchronously.
- **Verified** on build `9df703dd`: `calzoom` 26/26, `mvmenu` 89/89, `labelsetup` 41/41, `scenariofacts` 12/12,
  `zoomresize` 40/40, `sideboxes` 78/78, `phasenum` 24/24 and 31/31; `npm run check` 40/40. No full gate: one
  chrome rule removed, outside `#table-wrap`.

### ✅ 26 Oct 2026: THE MONTH VIEW SCROLLS IN ITS OWN BOX, SO THE TOOLBAR STAYS PUT AT ANY ZOOM. ✅ LIVE at `71a6c87` (committed and pushed on the owner's picker; the live page served it ~70 s later, byte-identical to the gated build `977b3ea6`)

- **Asked** (owner): *"The viewport of the month view needs to work the same way as the viewport of the waterfall
  view. When you zoom in with the slider, it needs to zoom in just the viewport of the calendar and the header only.
  not the surrounding menus. The surrounding menus need to stay fixed in place."* Tracker card `month-zoom-viewport`.
- **Cause:** the month view had no scroll box. It scrolled in `.preview-panel`, so the toolbar and the text style
  bar scrolled with it. Zoomed in (`#table-wrap` was `width: calc(100% * z)`), they slid sideways off screen too.
- **Built** (no frozen code; `#table-wrap`'s own box only, nothing inside it, no `.mv-*` rule):
  - `legacy.css`, in the app shell's `(width > 960px)` block: in the month view (`body:has(#view-month-btn.active)`,
    the side boxes' selector) the panel is a flex column. `#table-wrap` gets `flex:1 1 0; min-height:0;
    overflow:auto` and becomes the scroll box. Zoomed, it is a one-track grid,
    `calc(100% * var(--cal-zoom))`, `align-content:start`, which pins `.month-view` to its 100% width as the
    inline width did. Stacked (≤960) keeps the old width rule, as CSS now.
  - `app.js` `applyCalZoom()` writes `--cal-zoom` on `#table-wrap`, never the width. In `setCalZoom()` the
    month's "hold the corner" block is gone: a scroll offset inside the zoomed box is in its own px, so the corner
    stays put (measured: a cell 75.75 px down at 150% is 101 px down at 200%, the same content point).
  - Frozen `captureScroll()`/`restoreScroll()` already keep `#table-wrap`'s scroll across renders (by id).
    `revealMonthCell()` finds the first scrolling ancestor, so it now scrolls `#table-wrap`, zoom-aware already.
- ⚠️ **Tests that scrolled the panel in the month view now scroll `#table-wrap`:** `mvmenu` (bringIntoView, L8, C1's
  synthetic scroll), `labelsetup`, `phasenum`. `calzoom` Z5/M1/M2 now measure `#table-wrap` / `.month-view`. New
  M2b: at 150%, scrolled to the far corner, the toolbar row and the box are where they were at 100%, and the panel has
  no scroll.
- **Verified:** toolbar and box positions identical between views at 100% (83 / 175 px at 1440×900); 900 px
  stacked layout unchanged (pinned width, page scrolls sideways). Full gate on build `977b3ea6`: **3,074 PASS,
  3 FAIL**, all on `tests/fixtures/stintswap-reshape.sptcal`. That file had UNCOMMITTED edits (+378/−30) in the main
  checkout before this session began; they are not this session's. `stintreshape` (grid never renders) and `phasesui` P1
  (stintswap-reshape controls/fields.byId) fail identically on a build of unchanged HEAD. Gate 13's
  stintswap-reshape month PDF was not re-run on HEAD, but it reads the same edited fixture. Every other baseline
  comparison passed. `npm run check` 40/40.
- **Shipped:** owner's picker, *"Commit"* and *"Commit and push"*. Only this session's files were committed. The
  edited `stintswap-reshape.sptcal` and the untracked `Reshape Fixture 2026-09-29.sptcal` are still uncommitted in the
  main checkout, belonging to whoever made them. The push also carried `a81aad1` (HANDOFF only).
- ⚠️ **For whoever owns that fixture edit:** with it, `stintreshape`, `phasesui` P1 and gate 13 (sr) fail. Either
  the edit is unfinished, or the baselines need re-cutting with the owner's sign-off.

  session. Plan §5 says what was built, card by card, and where it differs from the plan's letter.
- **Part 1, Hide in waterfall.** `phaseHidden` (`{key: {sheet: true}}`, absent when nothing is hidden), read only
  through `phaseHiddenIn()`. ⚠️ **Not quite §2.2's mechanism, and the reason matters:** feeding `wfSegments` to the
  padding alone would drop a hidden phase's weeks from `schedule.weeks`, which the MONTH view also reads (its notes
  are looked up by week in that list, and its "Week N" comes from `seg.weekNo`, numbered in the week loop). So the loop
  still spans and numbers every phase; only cells are left out, and the waterfall's rows are cut out with the calendar
  range's own `cutToCalendarRange()` (the whole list kept as `fullRange`, which `update()` already hands the month
  view). `hidewf` W1-W3: hidden = not scheduled, exactly, in the waterfall, the workbook and the waterfall PDF; M1: the
  month view byte-identical hidden or not.
- **Part 2, the blend.** The engine's own generators and `legacy.css`, no React. The colour square is still
  `#swatch-<key>` (first child; CSS places it on the head); the head reads `--phase-color`, written by ONE helper,
  `paintPhaseSwatch()`, that every swatch write now goes through; `#meta-` / `#unitmeta-` moved into the head. Switches
  are CSS on the native checkboxes (`.sw-row`). Production's box is `.prod-total-block` over `.ep-panel`, joined by CSS.
  The All-phase hiatus card gained a dates line (`reflectHiatusRanges()`). UI-CONVENTIONS §5 updated for R7.

  fixtures, the Phases card's id'd controls in order, the saved `fields.byId` and hiatus rows from the build BEFORE the

    tag shown while the card is folded.

    still messy with hiatus and sim post"*. The refined A is in the mockup (left column, marked Picked) and spelled
    out in plan §3.4. Every hiatus and Sim Post is now the same one-line "option box". Production reads: Starts,
    then SHOOTING ORDER with the total on its heading, then the two boxes. Episodic Prep takes the same shape.

    `--phase-color` that the colour writes set on the row. Plan §3.4a.

### 🔨 23 Oct 2026 (latest): THE KEY FACTS BAND CENTRED OVER THE CALENDAR (option A) — branch `scenario-facts` on top of live `1ab777c`; TARGETED LEGS GREEN; NOT PUSHED (owner: *"then ask me before pushing"*)

- **Why:** the window-centred band (B, live at `1ab777c`) drew *"how is this centered?"* from the owner, with a
  screenshot. Measured on it: the window's middle was 1,000 of 2,000 px and the bar's middle about 1,006, so it was
  centred exactly as B specified. But the calendar box runs 513–1,988, middle about 1,250. **The window's centre reads
  as off-centre whenever the sidebar is on screen**, as the A/B mock had warned. Owner: *"Switch the key facts bar to
  centre over the calendar area (option A), then ask me before pushing."*
- **Built:** `placeBand()` now does `left + max(0, (area − width) / 2)`, where the area is `left` to
  `innerWidth − right`, the calendar's edges. B's sidebar clamp is gone: A can't reach the sidebar. CSS and comments
  are updated.
- **Proof:**
  - `scenariofacts` 12/12. F12 now measures against the calendar area, panel left + 28 to the panel's content
    right − 28: offset 0.
  - `scenarios` 22/22, `npm run check` 40/40, build `ce675778`.
  - Browser pane at 1000 px (offset 0), and resized live to 1440 px (offset 1).
- ⏭ **Next:** ask the owner before pushing (they asked for that). Local `main` still owes the merge.
- 📝 **Lesson:** a literal "centre in the window" on a layout with a sidebar is rarely what's meant. Say so plainly at
  the mock stage, as the recommendation did, and when it is chosen anyway, show it at the owner's own window size
  before shipping.

### 🔨 23 Oct 2026 (latest): THE KEY FACTS BAND CENTRED IN THE WINDOW — branch `scenario-facts` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/scenario-facts`) on top of live `f29f268`; TARGETED LEGS GREEN; ✅ **LIVE at `1ab777c`**

- **Asked** (owner): *"what if we centered the bar left to right in the window"*. Mocked in chat: A over the calendar
  (recommended) or B in the whole window. **Chosen: B**, *"Centre the key facts band in the whole window, sidebar
  included"*. Tracker: `key-facts-band-centred`.
- **Built** (`ScenarioTabs.jsx` + `legacy.css` only):
  - `.sc-band` is `width:max-content`, with a `max-width` of the calendar's width;
  - `placeBand()` writes its `left`: the window's centre, CLAMPED to the calendar's area (`left`..`innerWidth - right`,
    the edges the band used to span);
  - it runs on every render, on the band's own resize, and on `window` `resize`. The strip doesn't re-render on a
    window resize (its measured edges don't change), so without that listener the band would stay off-centre.
- ⚠️ **The clamp is the owner's B with one guard.** Below about 1,110–1,240 px, depending on the sidebar's width, the
  window's centre would put the band's left end on the sidebar, so it stops at the calendar's left edge. Seen at 1000
  and 961 px: left = calendar left, 308.
- **Proof:**
  - `scenariofacts` 12/12. F12 is new (centre within 1 px of the window's, left ≥ the calendar's); F9 now narrows the
    band through `max-width`.
  - `scenarios` 22/22, `npm run check` 40/40, build `f01a1115`.
  - Browser pane at 1440 / 1280 (centred, offset 0) and 1000 / 961 (clamped).
- ✅ **PUSHED AND LIVE** (owner, by picker: *"Push and deploy"*). ONE `git push origin scenario-facts:main` at 21:31:10

### 🔨 23 Oct 2026 (latest): NO PURPLE FLASH BETWEEN THE MONTH VIEW'S "+" BUTTONS — committed on local `main` as `e11beb4`, then replayed alone onto `origin/main` (`8c78372`, the key facts band) on branch `mv-gap-flash` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/mv-gap-flash`), because local `main` holds another session's uncommitted work and the owner chose *"Push only the fix"*; FULL GATE GREEN for this change (the 2 FAILs are a modified fixture, below)

- **Asked** (owner): *"as you scroll up and down over a square in the month view, the square flashes purple for a
  split second as your cursor hovers in between the tiny gap between the selector buttons for comments. this is
  distracting and feel unprof"*. On the tracker as `month-gap-hover-flash`.
- **Cause, measured at 1440×900:** the purple is `#table-wrap .mv-daycell:hover` (the day-menu cue, owner-approved
  frozen edits of 18 and 21 Sep). `.mv-bars` is `pointer-events:none`, so below the number band the CELL is the hit
  target in every hole the bar layer leaves: the 2 px lane gaps (`gap:2px 0`), the 1 px seams of `margin:0 1px`, the
  3 px side padding, the strip above `.mv-row-expand`. `elementFromPoint` returned `.mv-daycell` in all of them.
- **Built** (`src/styles/legacy.css`, after `#table-wrap .mv-out:hover .mv-daynum`): ONE frozen CSS rule,
  `#table-wrap .mv-daygrid::after{ content:''; position:absolute; left:0; right:0; top:24px; bottom:0; }`. A
  transparent sheet over the day grid below the band, painted after the cells and under `.mv-bars` (z-index 1). The
  holes now hit `.mv-daygrid`, which is no `.mv-daycell`: no highlight, and a LEFT-click or double-click there does
  nothing (it used to open the day menu / clear a mark). The band, every "+", note, pill and the row-expand bar are
  unchanged. Right-click is unchanged too, because `dayContextAt` finds the day by point inside `.mv-body`.
  - ⚠️ `top:24px` is `.mv-bars`' `padding-top` (the band). Change one, change both.
  - Paints nothing, takes no space (inside the out-of-flow `.mv-daygrid`), and cannot print: `#print-root` is not
    inside `#table-wrap`, and the month-PDF writer reads no DOM.
- **Verified:**
  - In the browser pane (dev server, 1440×900): `elementFromPoint` in every hole now returns `.mv-daygrid`. A real
    mouse sweep down one day lit the band and left all seven gap positions grey. A left-click on the band opens the
    day menu. A left-click in a gap does nothing, a right-click there opens that day's menu, and "+" still opens the
    note editor.
  - `npm run check` 40/40. Twelve month legs on the build, each with the gate's fixture: `mvmenu` 89/89,
    `labelsetup` 41/41, `halfworked` 24/24, `overrides` 20/20, `mvnotes` 30/30 and 14/14, `epnames` 10/10,
    `halfdays` 27/27, `mvhdroff` 9/9, `monthnotes` 7/7, `monthlanes` 6/6, `offweek` and `calzoom` pass.
  - **Full gate** on build `57204bbd…` (port 8239, 20:24–20:53, no other gate running): **3,035 PASS, 2 FAIL.**
    Both FAILs are on `stintswap-reshape` (`stintreshape` timed out, and gate 13 said "THE CONTENT MOVED").
    ⚠️ **That fixture is MODIFIED in this checkout and was before this work began.** Its mtime is 21 Oct 18:17, and
    25 keys differ from HEAD: an app save seems to have landed on it. Not mine, and left untouched, as was the
    untracked `Reshape Fixture 2026-09-29.sptcal`. **On the committed fixture** (a temporary copy under another name,
    deleted after): gate 13's written month PDF is byte-identical to its baseline (102,988 bytes), and `stintreshape`
    passes every check. Every other frozen output matched its baseline, gates 10 and 13 included.
- **Owner's pickers, 23 Oct 2026:** *"Push only the fix"* (not the Phases plan commit `5cc8286`, which stays on local
  `main`), and *"Leave it for now"* for the modified `stintswap-reshape.sptcal`. ⚠️ Until that fixture is restored
  (`git checkout -- tests/fixtures/stintswap-reshape.sptcal`, the owner's call), every full gate in the main checkout

  - **A ResizeObserver alone didn't move the grid in the leg.** The page wasn't painting (hidden pane, headless
    virtual time), so its callback never came: the band wrapped to 48 px and its room stayed at 38. So the band
    re-measures on every React render, and the grid fit now also observes `<html>`'s `style` attribute as a
    mutation. Same lesson as the grid fit's own notice-strip comment.
  - **`var`, not `let`, for `liveScenarioFacts` / `BAND_KEYS` / the timers:** `pushScenarioChrome()` can run during
    evaluation, before those lines, and a TDZ error would take the whole IIFE (the `scenarioTabs` rule).
  - **A flip refreshes the band synchronously** (`switchScenario`). On the debounce alone the band showed the tab
    just left for 120 ms.
  - **`fields.byId` stores `{value}` per id**, not the bare value.
  - **Dimmed is `--text-muted`, Compare's `dimmed`, not the mockup's lighter `#A5A197`**, which was about 2:1 on the
    band's `gray-1`.
  - **The after-Load probe** runs a few tabs per tick, waits while a note editor is open or a field is focused, and
    banks a pending edit first, as Compare does. A tab that fails is marked `factsFailed` and skipped. Until every tab
    is known, nothing is dimmed.

    Measured in the real build at 1440 px: the bar is 1038 px wide, its controls end at x≈620 and the hint
    (`.style-bar:not(.has-target)::after`) at ≈830. The facts need ~613 px plus the label. **F fits windows from about
    1,277 px once the hint gives way, and from about 1,488 px with the hint.** It costs no calendar height. Below
    about 1,280 px it needs a fallback (not designed). The facts end up far from the tabs (top vs bottom).
  - **Values the same in every scenario are grey** (Compare's grey-row rule); what differs stays dark. A first draft
    tinted differing values yellow, but realistic scenarios differ in most facts, so everything went yellow.
  - **Location box** (optional text, under Production Region in Show info): the line and Compare's Location row show it.
    When it's blank they show the region SHORTENED ("Manitoba", "Toronto", "U.S. (Area Standards)"), not the list's
    full label. It never changes a date.
- ⚠️ **If built, Location is a NEW saved field:** an id'd Show info control lands in `fields.byId`, so its id is
  permanent from the first save (CLAUDE.md "Every saved calendar must keep opening"). An old file without it shows the
  region. Per scenario, like every Show info field. The values should come from `scenarioFacts()` (the header's own
  tokens), so the line, the header and Compare cannot disagree.

### 🔨 23 Oct 2026 (latest): COMPARE WINDOW FITS ITS TABLE — on branch `scenarios` on top of live `93836f5`; ✅ **LIVE at `acdae89`**; targeted checks green; COMMITTED and PUSH APPROVED by the owner's pickers (*"Commit"*, *"Push and deploy"*)

- **Asked** (owner): *"the window needs to scale larger so we dont have elipses/things cut off"*. On the tracker as
  `compare-window-fit`.
- **Reproduced:** at 1440×900, a 36-character scenario name ended in "…" in its heading and the location wrapped,
  while the window was 776 px wide.
- **Built** (`src/chrome/CompareDialog.jsx`):
  - the table is `width: max-content`, with each column held to a minimum (`COL_MIN`, `LABEL_MIN`) and nothing
    wrapping;
  - a `ResizeObserver` on the table (`measure`) sizes the Modal. It reads `offsetWidth`, never
    `getBoundingClientRect()`, because the opening transition scales the content;
  - `xOffset` / `yOffset` 24;
  - a 12 px bottom pad on the scroll container, so an overlay scrollbar no longer sits on the last row.
  - `exportCompareExcel` sizes its columns from the text.
- ⚠️ **Mantine caps a Modal at 90% of the window** (`xOffset` 5vw) whatever its `size`. Pass `xOffset` to go wider.
- **Verified:**
  - 3 scenarios: 903 px, nothing clipped (every `p` and badge label has scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth);
  - 12 scenarios: 1,392 px, and the 2,511 px table scrolls;
  - `scenarios` 22/22, `npm run check` 40/40.
- ✅ **PUSHED AND LIVE.** ONE `git push origin main` at 16:59:20 (`93836f5..acdae89`, which carried the docs commit
  `0862d6b` too). The deploy succeeded at 17:00:05. The live `index.html` has the same sha256 as the tested
  `dist/index.html` (`3823d4d8…`). No version cut.

  lines; it is 4 now. **The test every item had to pass** (plan §1.1): it is what you reach for on THAT cell, and the
  cell has no one-gesture way to do it already (a double-click edits or expands, Delete clears, ⌥← swaps). Plan §1.2
  lists what was cut and why. Saved as a feedback memory (`lean-right-click-menus`).
- **Ruled by picker, 23 Oct 2026:** R5 *"Both"* (a week offers Add 1-week phase here and Add hiatus; the
  recommendation was Neither); R6 *"Leave it out"* (an item that cannot act on the cell is left out, not greyed;
  Start / End stay a greyed pair); R7 *"Trim the month menu too"*, as its own task after this one (tracker card
  `month-menu-trim`, requested; NOT this branch).
- **What it is** (plan §2): phase week = Start / End ‹phase› this week, Add 1-week phase here, Add hiatus ▸; Notes
  cell = Cut · Copy · Paste · Clear (one row) and Back to automatic text on an edited automatic note; band = Remove
  hiatus…; date / empty / Sim Post = the two adds; a highlight of several = the clipboard row; Size only after a hand
  resize; last line Show this day / week in the month view (no undo step, a ring over the day for 1.7 s). No menu on
  a heading (double-click edits it), the title block, or with Shift.
- **How:** inside `installMonthDayMenu`, one engine with the month menu (`wfContextAt`, `wfPrepare`,
  `wfMenuSections`, the `wf*` items). No frozen code, no snapshot key, no id. Calls `setPhaseStart` (in
  `asShootDayMove`), `phaseEdgePlan` / `phaseEdgeWrite` / `phaseEdgeLanded`, the month menu's `addPhaseHere`,
  `hiatusSections` (now told a span: 7 days in the waterfall, overlap like the scheduler's rows), `removeHiatusHere`,
  `wfNotesCopy` / `wfNotesClear` / `wfNotesPaste`, `wfNoteStep`. End on a Weeks phase keeps Weeks and gets a new
  count (no switch to Dates); Start moves by whole weeks, keeping the weekday. Cut writes `navigator.clipboard` BEFORE
  it clears, so a refused clipboard never loses the note (not `wfNotesCut()`, which clears first). The Mac Ctrl+click
  guard is now on the text highlight's and the phase selection's presses. `.day-menu` is capped at the window height
  and scrolls inside (the month menu too).
- ⚠️ **Learned, measured: Chrome can deliver a scroll event LATE, with nothing moved.** In the leg (headless, virtual
  time) a scroll event arrived after the menu opened, either for the grid's box at the exact position it had at the
  right-click, or for the SIDEBAR settling after an earlier undo, and closed the menu: a different case each run, and
  a fixed sleep or "wait until quiet" did not cure it. So the WATERFALL menu now closes only when a box around its
  cell really moved (`scrollBoxes` / `movedSinceOpen`), or on a render. ⛔ The month menu keeps "any scroll outside
  it" (`mvmenu` C1 dispatches an unmoved scroll and expects a close); change it in its own trim, not here. In the app
  pane, a real browser, no scroll fired after a right-click on any of four cell kinds.

### ✅ 23 Oct 2026 (latest): HEADER: OFF FOR THE MONTH VIEW — built in the main checkout as `1af8782`; ✅ **LIVE at `d25eaf6`**, carried by ANOTHER session's combined landing (the waterfall right-click menu, owner-approved push); this session pushed nothing; no version cut

- **Asked** (owner, answering the scope-lines picker below): *"delete it too, but also add "Off" for the month view
  header"*. On the tracker as `mv-header-off`. Ruling 10 (20 Oct) had given Off to the waterfall only.
- **Built, with no frozen function edited** (CLAUDE.md's state table has the row):
  - app.js: `mvHeaderOff` beside `headerOff`. `setMvHeaderMode()` now treats Off the way `setHeaderMode()` does: a
    flag on top of the mode, so Off → the mode underneath brings the lines back, and each switch is one
    `asOneHeaderStep`. `headerModeKey(true)` returns 'off', the menu's Off row lost `sheetOnly`, the template editor
    and a month preset's Apply clear it, and New clears it. `captureSnapshot` writes `mvHeaderOff: true` only while it
    is on, `sanitizeSnapshot` drops a non-boolean, and `applyStateSnapshot` restores it unconditionally (`=== true`).
  - Screen and print rollback: `reflectHeaderOff()` also toggles `body.mv-hdr-off`, and legacy.css COLLAPSES
    `.mv-header` (height 0, no margin, padding, shadow or fill, `visibility:hidden`). ⛔ **Never `display:none`.**
    Frozen `exportMonthPdf` measures each page's reserve as the first week's top minus `.mv-header`'s top, with the
    pages stacked in an off-screen host. A `display:none` header's rect is all zeros, so every page after the first
    gets a distance of hundreds of px, fails the `< PAGE_H` test and falls back to 105.
  - The direct writer: `exportMonthPdfDirect` stamps `headerOff: true` on each month of the model, between
    `buildMonthLayout` and `fitMonthLayout`, only while it is on (so default layouts and bytes are unchanged). `mvlFrame`
    then uses an empty header ending at `y0`. It reads the month, never the engine, so the writer's slices stay
    self-contained. ⛔ **Never inside `buildMonthLayout`:** the first cut stamped it there, and `monthlayout` (all four
    runs) failed with "could not locate the layout model (MVL_GEOMETRY..buildMonthLayout): re-anchor this leg". The
    leg slices that function up to its last statement, verbatim. It is back to byte-identical, and the stamp is a
    plain loop in the export, which adds no writer name to the F0 checks (`monthlayout`, `monthemit`, `monthwriter`).
  - `mvHeaderLine()` is deliberately unchanged (unlike the waterfall's `headerLine()`, which returns '' while Off):
    nothing that prints reads it while the header is collapsed, and the Template → Manual bake must read real lines.
  - Help page: the Header paragraph names Off and what it does in each view. PreviewToolbar's month tooltip mentions Off.
- **Legs:** new `mvhdroff` (in gate.sh's AFSPEC list, 400 s), M0–M7 + E0. `hdrrow` O1 now expects Off in BOTH menus.
- **Proof so far,** build `c7b90c4f…`: `mvhdroff` 9/9. M2: the reserve is 119 → 72 px on all 16 pages, the same 47 px
  the month bar moves on screen (M1). M3: the written PDF has none of the header's lines and its first month label sits

### ✅ 23 Oct 2026: MONTH VIEW — UNTOUCHED NOTES KEEP THEIR BAR, NOTES BETWEEN PHASES, DRAG A NOTE TO ANOTHER DAY — branch `month-drag-fix` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/month-drag-fix`), from `main` at `8cc8357`; TWO FROZEN EDITS (E3, E4), OWNER-APPROVED BY PICKER; COMBINED FULL GATE 2,974 / 0 (`d34f917a`); ✅ **LIVE at `2c6a822`** (verified byte-identical); in local `main` since `cc9a8ad`, where the Header: Off session merged `origin/main` on top of its unpushed `1af8782` ([`MONTH-ORDER-PLAN.md`](MONTH-ORDER-PLAN.md) §9)

- **Asked:** *"you need to really refine and debug the month view drag to resort order of phases/notes. its buggy and
  sometimes when you move one phase or note it moves others in adjacent days. obviously when you drag a multi week
  phase up or down its going to move that phase to the same position on every week but notes need to be able to
  individually move in or around phases on a per day basis. Additionally, i want the ability to drag notes from one
  day to another and for auto notes or notes with dates, have the date auto adjust"*. Tracker cards
  `month-reorder-fixes` and `month-note-day-drag`.
- ⭐ **The cause (reproduced, both triggers):** frozen `renderMonthView` places every note first-fit, so an untouched
  note takes the first free line of its DAY. Anything that pushes a week's bars down a line opens a line above the
  bars on that week's OTHER days, and their notes rise into it. (1) A note dragged on top of Wed 3/4: "Tue note" and
  "Thu note" jumped above the bars. (2) Prod Prep's grip dragged to the top in the week of 3/15: the order is the whole
  calendar's (D1), so the week of 3/8, where Prod Prep runs Thu–Fri only, re-stacked and its Mon–Wed notes jumped above
  both bars.
- **The fix, no new state:** once a calendar holds any order or note place (`mvIsArranged()`), each month render first
  asks the frozen renderer for the UNTOUCHED layout (`mvRefMode`: date order, no places), reads off the bar directly
  above every note on its day (`mvReferenceAnchors`), and places each untouched note no higher than the line under that
  bar. A note with no bar above it there is placed as before. A never-rearranged calendar takes the old path exactly.
  Render cost on a rearranged calendar: ~6 → ~15 ms per month.
- **New, per the ask:** a dragged note lands above every bar (`top`), under a phase (`under: '<key>'`) or under every
  bar (`bottom: true`), and the day it lands on is written note by note at the places they are drawn. A note can go to
  ANOTHER day: dates naming its old day are rewritten (any typed form); a waterfall line moves into the new week's
  waterfall note through `saveNoteEdit`, its trailing date rewritten or stamped; an auto-note so moved becomes an edit
  (its phase does not move). Defaults taken, plan §9.2 D9–D12: holidays stay on their day; a drop >24 px off the grid
  puts the note back; waterfall lines only into weeks the waterfall has; one place per waterfall-line text.
- **Frozen edits (picker, 23 Oct: *"Approve with that gate"*):** E3, `takeLane`/`place` take an optional `minLane`, and
  the notes loop passes it plus `data-note-anchor` (the row index of the bar above); E4, each phase's iteration ends by
  placing the notes under it (`placeNotes('u:' + key, notesFrom)`). The month-PDF writer (not frozen) reads the anchor
  and starts that note's re-pack search under the same item.
- **Save format:** `under` / `bottom` on a `dayNotes` entry; `under` / `bottom` / `day` on an `mvWfNotePlace` value.
  `day` fixes a second, smaller bug: a place was keyed by text alone, so one arrangement moved every same-text line
  (yearly holidays, a line typed into several weeks). Sanitized and restored field by field; no `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` bump.
- **The note drag was rewritten** (`installMonthNoteDrag`): every redraw starts from the stores as they were at the
  press (`dayNotes`, `userNotes`, `mvWfNotePlace`, `noteColors` copied), so nothing accumulates; a drop that draws the
  month exactly as before (out and back, Escape) restores the very state and banks no step.
- **Proof so far (build `95bf3fd4…`, 1,763,999 bytes; commits `51ecdc1`, `6cd0d37`, `81c7ffc`):** `npm run check`
  40/40; `mvorder` 30/30 with M1–M10 on the new synthetic `tests/fixtures/mvdrag.sptcal` (M3 reads the WRITTEN month
  PDF: the between-phases note's y lies between its two bars'); on the live build `d2ac914e` M1–M6 and M8 are red.
  `pilldrag` 30/30, `phaseedge` 23/23, `hiatusdrag` 13/13. In the pane: a between-phases drop and a Tue→Thu drop with
  the date rewritten. N3's expectation now includes the place's `day`.
- ⚠️ **Learned: a harness probe that measures an element BEFORE a drag and uses it AFTER gets a zero rect** (the redraw
  replaced it), so its "drop" lands at (0, 0). It looked like a bug (a note landed on Sun 3/1) and was the probe's. It
  did expose a real gap, fixed as D10.
- ✅ **Full gate on `95bf3fd4…`** (1,763,999 bytes; port 9807, 13:27:37–13:56:38, alone: the scenarios session's gate
  ended 13:26, the next began 13:57): **2,970 PASS, 1 FAIL.** All 90 month-PDF lines (gate 10's print documents, gate
  13's written PDFs) identical to the baselines, which is the R4/§9.5 proof that E3 and E4 are inert on every calendar
  never rearranged; `mvorder` 30/30. The FAIL: `loadcarry` stopped after C4 ("EX: harness") in C5, which waits up to 20 s
  for a legacy `.html` to Load. Alone on the same build, twice: 6/6, no errors. A Load-timing flake (the `offweek` R8
  kind), not this change, which does not touch the Load path or that leg's views.
- `gate.sh`'s `mvorder` comment and label now name M1–M10 and `mvdrag` (comment only; edited after the gate).
- ⏭ **Next:** the owner's picker on merging into `main` and, separately, on pushing.

- **Landing (owner's pickers):** *"Merge into local main"* and *"Push after the combined gate"*. `main` had moved (three
  live Settings changes, `06c8770`..`5a3d5da`), so it was merged into the branch as `2c6a822` (HANDOFF / README top
  entries both kept). `npm run check` 40/40. ✅ **Combined full gate on `d34f917a…`** (1,761,781 bytes; port 9807,
  14:24:57–14:53:30, after the main checkout's own gate): **2,974 PASS, 0 FAIL, GATE PASSED**; all 90 month-PDF lines
  identical to the baselines, `mvorder` 30/30.
- ⚠️ **Then local `main` moved AGAIN** during that gate: another session committed `1af8782` "Header: Off for the Month

- ✅ **PUSHED AND LIVE:** ONE `git push origin HEAD:main` from the worktree at 14:55:12 (`01793cf..2c6a822`, 7 commits:
  the Settings session's HANDOFF note `5a3d5da` plus this work). Deploy run 40030164760 succeeded; at 14:56:24 the live

    `--sw-rail-h` takes the same off its height (legacy.css). It is measured from the rail's on-screen bottom edge,

    `aria-label="Settings"`. Close = the Settings button (a toggle), Show / Phases, or Escape from inside.

    gives it up (its class stays: that is where Show / Phases go back to). At 960 px and below the rail now stays

    0 clipped, 76 ids.
- **`hiatuslabel` test fix (`11a041b`, after the gate, test only).** `pdfHiatusRuns()` trimmed 0x0A / 0x0D off every
  PDF stream, but a compressed stream can END in one of those bytes. On 23 Oct the header date 10.23.26 made
  `origin/main`'s waterfall stream do that, and N1-2 counted 0 "Hiatus" on every build. The paper-recommend session
  found it. The leg now cuts at the dictionary's `/Length`, as `pdfdialog.js` does, and passes 7/7 alone on the gated
  build. The gate's own run passed without the fix only because the 4 pt header clearance shifts the stream bytes.
  ⚠️ `calrange`, `hdrtemplate`, `hdrrow`, `notewrap`, `onecol`, `prodspan` and `winansi` still trim, and can fail the
  same way on another date.
- ⚠️ **Learned: `HARNESS_PORT` is not reserved between sessions.** This session and the paper-recommend session both
  ran legs on 9771 at 10:30–10:38. They shared the server port and the `/tmp/tc9771-*` Chrome profiles, so
  `notescols`, `sideboxes` and `wfkeysreal` "produced no result" / "timed out waiting for the waterfall". All three
  passed at once on 9790. Before picking a port, check `lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN`. A "no result" with no
  assertion failure is a clash or contention until proven otherwise.
- ⚠️ **paper-recommend's merged-build gate (2,751 / 5) is explained in its own entry.** None of the five is the
  feature's: three were contention, one the R16 flake, one the `hiatuslabel` date bug.

  cache-buster, was **byte-identical to the gated build** (sha256 `d2ac914ed7155929…`, 1,755,411 bytes, `cmp`).
  `version.json` is still 1.8.0, so installed copies pick it up silently; a version cut is the owner's call. This

### ✅ 5 Oct 2026 (evening), landed 23 Oct: THE WATERFALL PDF HEADER LEAVES 4 pt ABOVE THE GRID — a FROZEN EDIT, owner-approved; branch `wf-header-clear` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/month-baselines`), COMMITTED `a71514c` and `main` (`59dd0cd`) MERGED IN as `d950b78` on the owner's *"merge … into main"* (23 Oct 2026); IN LOCAL `main` via `land-ready-1023` (combined gate 2,962 / 0, 23 Oct), NOT PUSHED

- **The ask.** Looking at a waterfall PDF, the owner asked two things:
  - why there is more white space between the header's left section and its centre;
  - for more white space between the centre's bottom line and the grid.

  A first answer was about the MONTH PDF's header, by mistake. Then the owner said "my bad i meant for the waterfal
  cal".
- **The left gap: explained, left alone** (owner's picker: "Leave it, matching Excel"). The waterfall header IS
  Excel's three-section header (&L/&C/&R):
  - left at the grid's left edge;
  - the centre centred on the page;
  - the right ending at the grid's right edge.

  A short date on the left and three long lines on the right leave about 310 pt against 170 pt of white. An Excel
  print shows the same.
- **The bottom gap: changed** (picker: "More clearance, PDF only", then "Yes: re-cut and gate it" after a before and
  after of the owner's own calendar). The header lives in Excel's top-margin band (21.6 pt to 54 pt), and three lines
  are shrunk to fit it. The band used to leave 1 pt; it now leaves 4 pt (`HDR_CLEAR_PT` in `buildWaterfallPdf`, with
  a ⛔ FROZEN EDIT comment).
  - The cost: a three-line header's type is about 10% smaller (×0.904; title 9.025 → 8.163 pt, the other lines
    6.564 → 5.936 pt).
  - Unchanged: the grid, Excel, a header that fits already, and the month PDF.
- **State:** branch `wf-header-clear` in `.claude/worktrees/month-baselines`, cut from `main` at `c7017a9`. It sat
  UNCOMMITTED for 18 days: the owner's commit/push picker was interrupted when the app quit on 5 Oct. On 23 Oct the
  owner asked to *"merge 'More space under the waterfall PDF header' into main"* (the tracker card's name). So:
  - committed as `a71514c`;
  - `main` at `59dd0cd`, 400 commits on, merged in as `d950b78`. Only HANDOFF and README conflicted (both top
    entries kept). `git diff main` over `src`, `tests` and `tools` is exactly this change: `HDR_CLEAR_PT`, the re-cut
    `base.pdf` with its `.json`, and the baseline README's section;
  - main had not touched `base.pdf` since `c7017a9`, so the 5 Oct re-cut still stands. On the merged build
    (`2626faea…`), `base` alone (port 9811) matches it bar the stamp (`pdfcmp.py` exit 0), and differs from main's old
    baseline in the header only (exit 1, first diff `/F1 6.564 Tf` → `/F1 5.936 Tf`). `npm run check` 40/40;
  - ⛔ **NOT fast-forwarded into `main` by this branch, and NO full gate run on it here.** The owner's direction,
    relayed by the "Hide unused notes columns" session (23 Oct): *"i want this to merge into main along with all the
    other features we are trying to merge, then push everything at once"*. So `wf-header-clear` lands through the
    "Merge ready branches" session's `land-ready-1023`, with ONE full gate on the combination and ONE push;
  - ⚠️ **What that combined gate must show for this change:** gate 2 matches the RE-CUT `base.pdf` (bar the stamp).
    Any other leg that reads the waterfall PDF's header TYPE SIZE or the lowest line's position now sees ×0.904 on a
    three-line header (title 9.025 → 8.163 pt, other lines 6.564 → 5.936 pt). A FAIL there is this change, not a
    regression; legs written after 5 Oct (side boxes' band E5, paper size, header off, text styles, hide-notes P1–P3)
    have never run against it.
- **Proof:**
  - **The owner's downloaded PDF was byte-identical** to the harness's export of `stintswap-reshape` on the
    pre-change build, so the A/B shows exactly what the owner gets.
  - **A/B** on the reference, `stintswap-reshape`, `hdrtemplated` and `carry-rich`, decompressed: only 7 or 8
    header `Tj` operations differ, 0 others, and the font streams are identical. The lowest header text's bottom went
    from 53.0 to 50.0 pt (`pdftotext -bbox`), against the grid's 54 pt.
  - **Gate 2's `base.pdf` re-cut**, recorded in `tests/baselines/2026-08-29-stage-7/README.md`:
    - it was cut with the page's clock pinned to 29 Aug 2026, so it keeps the `BASEDATE` stamp, as the 29 Sep Excel
      re-cut did;
    - the pinned run equals today's run bar the stamp;
    - it differs from the old baseline by the 7 header lines only;
    - the new build passes it and the old build fails it (`pdfcmp.py` exit 1);
    - `base.pdf.txt` is identical, and `base.pdf.json` changed only in bytes, `contentLen` and `textXmax`.
  - **`npm run check`** 40/40.
  - ✅ **Full gate: 1,278 PASS, 0 FAIL, GATE PASSED,** on build `75db63a6…` (13 min 24 s). Gate 2 matches the re-cut baseline, every Excel part is identical, there are 0 clipped cells, `fields.byId` has 62 ids, and gate 13's month PDFs are byte-identical.
- ⭐ **Learned:**
  1. **"The header" is ambiguous between the two PDFs**, as "the grid" was (CLAUDE.md). Ask which PDF before
     answering.
  2. **The waterfall PDF's header type was ALREADY shrunk** from its nominal 11/8 pt to about 9.0/6.6 pt to fit three
     lines in the 32 pt band. Any more room under it costs type size, unless the band itself grows. Growing it means
     a smaller header margin or a larger top margin, both shared with Excel, so the Excel export would change too.
  3. **A PDF baseline with a date in a deflated stream** is re-cut by pinning the page's clock, not by editing bytes.

### 🔨 22–23 Oct 2026: COLUMN HEADINGS EDITABLE ALL THE TIME, THE SWITCH GONE — branch `claude/headings-editable-5c2c47` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/headings-editable-5c2c47`), cut from `hide-notes` at `7b3aa6c`; LEGS GREEN, FULL GATE ON THE COMBINED MERGE (`land-ready-1023`), NOT HERE; MERGED INTO LOCAL `main` via `land-ready-1023` (combined gate 2,962 / 0, 23 Oct), NOT PUSHED ([`HEADINGS-EDITABLE-PLAN.md`](HEADINGS-EDITABLE-PLAN.md))

- **Asked** (owner, mid-way through `hide-notes`): *"i say we remove the custom column headings toggle in settings and
  just make it editable all the time"*. Ruled by picker, 22 Oct: untouched calendars keep `Date | year | Notes`;
  switched-on files keep the year over Date; Q1 "Bring them back"; H1–H3 signed off. On the tracker as
  `headings-always-editable`.
- **Built:** `headerRowTyped()` (any label, any split, or `headerRowCustom`) replaces the switch as the path condition
  in the three frozen heading writers: H1 `renderSpreadsheetView`, H2 `exportExcel` row 1, H3 `buildWaterfallPdf`,
  one word each, commented `⛔ FROZEN EDIT H1/H2/H3`. `pdfUnprintablePlaces()` follows it. `headerRowDefault()` gives
  the Date column `Date` unless `headerRowCustom`. ONE double-click handler opens `openHeaderRowPop` on any heading,
  by `headingColKey(th)`; `headingCellFor(key)` re-finds a cell on either path after a button. The hover rule is
  `#table-wrap thead th:not(.wf-notes-strip)`. The Settings row, `reflectHeaderRowCustom`, `setHeaderRowCustom` and
  the Notes panel (`openNotesColPop`) are gone. New ⓘ text and a help-panel paragraph, *Column headings*.
- ⛔ **`headerRowCustom` is a PERMANENT snapshot key with a NEW meaning**: "saved with the old switch on, so the Date
  column defaults to the year". Still read, sanitized, restored and saved; nothing new writes it; New clears it.
  Never drop it: every switched-on file would lose its year over Date.
- ⚠️ **The plan's "Merge → on Date gives year over Date" was wrong** (plan §8). A merge keeps the text of the heading
  being edited, so the year over Date is **← Merge on the year heading**. Merge → on Date keeps "Date". The editor
  did not change; `hdrrow` H13 gates both.
- ⚠️ **Typing one heading no longer moves the year over Date.** On the typed path untyped headings now read Date,
  year, Notes, so the old "switch on → `[2026 over Date + phases]`" happens only for `headerRowCustom` files.
- **Proof:** `hdrrow` 37/37 (H1–H14 new; H9: a Reset of the last typed heading gives back row 1 of the workbook,
  every style, and the PDF content stream identical to an untouched export); `notescols` 49/49; `settingswin` 19/19
  (W13 asserts the switch is gone); each of the three red on the pre-change build (`f85c9814…`, `git archive` of
  `7b3aa6c`, served from an untracked `dist-pre/`). `textstyle` 51/51, `npm run check` 40/40. Browser pane, real
  mouse: the editor opened on an untouched Date and Notes heading, a typed heading changed only itself, undo put Date
  back, the Waterfall card has no switch.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** a double-click that lands while an undo is still re-rendering opens the editor and the
  `#table-wrap` observer closes it at once (its cell was replaced). That is by design (pattern 3); retry after the
  grid settles. The legs wait on `settle()` for this reason.
- ⏭ **Next: NOT merged from here.** Owner, 23 Oct 2026, relayed by the hide-notes session: *"i want this to merge
  into main along with all the other features we are trying to merge, then push everything at once"*. The "Merge
  ready branches" session (branch `land-ready-1023`) merges this branch with `wf-header-clear` and runs ONE full
  gate on the combination, so this branch runs no full gate of its own. `main` (with `hide-notes`, `02e7c9b`) was
  merged into this branch first (`9bdcc72`, docs only; build unchanged, `1cca748b…`). If the combined gate fails in
  `hdrrow`, `settingswin` or `notescols`, that is this branch's to fix. Never pushed.

### 📝 22 Oct 2026: SCENARIOS (SEVERAL VERSIONS OF ONE CALENDAR, COMPARED) — PLANNED AND RULED (D1–D6 all as recommended, by picker), NO CODE ([`SCENARIOS-PLAN.md`](SCENARIOS-PLAN.md), mockup [`mockups/scenarios.html`](mockups/scenarios.html))

- **The ask.** Item 8 of the Excel-gaps list. Owner: *"comparing multiple scenarios is HUGE"*. They want to know
  whether scenarios share one `.sptcal`, how you switch and compare, whether each is a version number, and how to keep
  it from getting complicated. The plan's §0 answers those four questions in a few lines.
- **What the plan recommends:**
  - one file holding up to 50 scenarios (owner, by picker, after the six rulings; the plan had said six). Tabs scroll
    sideways, and Compare shows six at a time, the tab on screen plus five you tick;
  - Excel-style tabs bottom-left of the calendar strip;
  - flip in place (same view, month and scroll; no flash, owner 22 Oct 2026: *"feely a little silly"*), plus a
    Compare table built from each tab's own header tokens (`buildHeaderCtx()`);
  - letters, not numbers ("Scenario A"), with Show Info's Version staying each tab's revision number;
  - nothing shared between tabs;
  - exports are of the tab you're on.
  - ✅ **Ruled 22 Oct 2026 by picker:** D1–D6 all as recommended. D6 includes the export-name frozen edit, approved
    in principle (the gate must show both files byte-identical).
  - ⏭ Next is step 1 of the plan's §10: the file format, with no UI.
- ⭐ **Facts found that the build must respect:**
  - **v1.1–v1.2 builds (including the root `index.html` rollback) accept ANY JSON object as a calendar**, with no
    version check and no `fields.byId` check.
    - That is why a multi-scenario file keeps the CURRENT tab's snapshot at the top level, with the other tabs under
      `scenarios`. Such a build then opens one tab, not a blank calendar it could save over everything.
    - Only files with 2+ tabs carry file `version: 2`, which v1.3.0–v1.8.0 refuse whole (`snapshotVersionOk`).
      One-scenario files stay byte-identical.
  - **The Excel and waterfall-PDF download names are built INSIDE frozen `exportExcel` / `exportWaterfallPdfDirect`.**
    Naming exports after the scenario is a one-line frozen edit (name only) and needs sign-off.
    `buildSavedFileName()` and `monthPdfFileName()` are free.
  - **`readState()` reads the DOM**, so Compare must probe each inactive tab synchronously (apply, compute, restore,
    never render), as `paperProbe` does. A gate leg must prove the current calendar is untouched.
  - `captureSnapshot()` carries `viewMode` and `sidebarTab`, so a switch must put back the current ones.
- **Mockup:** three synthetic calendars captured from `dist/` (`halfdays` plus two variants, built only in a scratchpad,
  no fixture added). Each has its waterfall plus its July–September 2026 month views (the Month button works), with
  working tabs and a tab menu.
- **The Compare window was restyled the same day.** It is a real Mantine component,
  `mockups/scenarios-compare/CompareDialog.jsx`, bundled into the mockup. Owner, after one round:
  - *"the styling of this new version but the content of the old version"*: every row and every value, the
    difference bold with a "+2 wks" tag, same rows grey;
  - *"remove all the random explainations"*: it is a table and its buttons, nothing else.
  - *"i do want to add the chip selector"*: chips pick the columns whenever there are three or more scenarios.
  - 23 Oct: *"compare up to 12 scenarios at once, scrolling left to right ... make the popup window larger ... export
    the table to excel or pdf"*. Now: up to twelve columns, a window that grows to the screen, pinned row labels, and
    Export to Excel / Export PDF.
    - In the mockup, Excel is a real ExcelJS 4.4.0 workbook.
    - The PDF prints through Save as PDF. The app would write it directly.

  Plan §2 has the rules.
  ⚠️ The mockup is ~890 KB, most of it the bundle and the captures. Weigh that before committing it.
- **Not committed.** Written in the main checkout by a side session. No `src/` change.

### 🔨 22 Oct 2026: RECOMMEND LETTER OR LEGAL IN THE EXPORT PDF DIALOG — branch `paper-recommend` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/paper-recommend`), from `main` at `55a9c61`; BUILT `f958f25`; ✅ FULL GATE 2,765 / 0 (`c6634df9`); THEN `origin/main` (`7856d98`) AND LOCAL `main` (`83ee71d`) MERGED IN as `b9f980f` + `a14c2e9`; FULL GATE ON THE MERGED BUILD 2,751 / 5 (`c27f8d58`), ALL FIVE EXPLAINED, NONE THIS BRANCH'S; MERGED INTO `land-ready-1023` BY THE "Merge ready branches" SESSION (at `a14c2e9`, then `f3a46f8`); IN LOCAL `main` via that landing (combined gate 2,962 / 0, 23 Oct), NOT pushed

- **Asked** (after v1.7.0's paper setting went live): *"could the app reccomend a paper size based on the waterfall
  cal? Really it should only reccomend between letter size and legal"*. Then *"Yes, build the Letter/Legal paper
  recommendation per PAPER-SIZE-PLAN.md §9 on a branch, and run the full gate. Don't merge or push."* Tracker card
  `paper-recommend`.
- ✅ **Ruled by picker, 22 Oct 2026:** P-R1 *"15% larger"*: Legal is Recommended when it prints more than 15% larger
  than Letter (the orientation rule's bar, strict), otherwise Letter; Tabloid never. P-R2 *"Mark it only"*: a tag in
  the paper control plus *"Legal prints this calendar about N% larger."*; the dialog still opens on the calendar's
  own paper, so Export at the defaults stays today's PDF.
- **Built** (no frozen code, no snapshot key, no format change):
  - `app.js`, beside `pageFitFor`: `PAPER_REC_BAR = 1.15` and `recommendPaper(fits)`, which returns `{ follow,
    portrait, landscape }`, each `{ paper, gain }`: each paper compared in the orientation the dialog would print it
    in. `askPdfPageSetup()` passes it as `paperRec`. The engine decides; React only picks the case for its
    orientation state (`orient || 'follow'`).
  - `ExportPdfDialog.jsx`: `SegLabel` takes an optional `tag`, a third line that is always rendered (a no-break
    space when empty) so the three paper segments stay one height; the line `.pdfdlg-paperrec` shows only while
    Legal is Recommended. Nothing selects a paper.
  - Help's *Exporting* paragraph: one clause.
- ⛔ **Test trap fixed first:** `t/pdfdialog.js`'s `recommended()` took the FIRST /Recommended/ label in the dialog.
  The paper row is above the orientation row, so a paper tag would silently have turned it into a paper reader. It
  now reads a tag by its segment's value (`taggedAll(values)`), with a twin `papersTagged()`.
- **Measured (the dialog's numbers, `pdfdialog`'s calendar):** one-year 87% Letter vs 96% Legal (+10%) → Letter;
  Single Column 67% vs 88% (+31%) → Legal; Single Column with Landscape clicked 50% vs 50% → Letter (the tag moves).

  save what the same drag saves at 100%, every handle on its row/column, and the full gate byte-identical.
- **Built.** Frozen edits, each commented in place as `⛔ FROZEN EDIT Zn`: Z1 `installGridResizers` (every rect length
  ÷ `gridZoom(wrap)`: column handle height, the table's offset, row-handle tops, span-handle top/height), Z2
  `spanHandleGeometry`, Z3 `repositionColHandles`, Z4 `repositionRowHandles`, Z5 `beginSpanDrag`'s pointer x, Z6 the
  column/row pointerdown (`startH` and both pointer deltas). `gridZoom()` is a hoisted declaration in "CALENDAR ZOOM";
  it is 1 at 100% and x / 1 is x exactly, so 100% cannot move. Non-frozen: the `body.cal-zoomed …
  .grid-resize-layer{display:none}` rule is gone (a comment says not to bring it back), `setCalZoom()`'s settle now
  keeps its `render()` at 100% and now calls `installGridResizers()` in the waterfall at every OTHER zoom (a zoomed
  layout's rows are not its 100% rows: 20.00 px at 200% vs ~20.84, so handles placed at one zoom drift at another).
  ⚠️ The first build replaced the 100% render with `installGridResizers()` alone and gated green (2,801 / 0), but the
  `textsel-zoom` session pointed out the text-style highlight relies on that render to snap back at 100%, so the
  render was put back before anything was pushed (`calzoom` M5's wording with it), the zoom
  button's tooltip no longer says "resize at 100%", `selGeom`'s comment updated.
- ⚠️ **Two things found that are not new, now visible:** (1) the last row's handle hangs 3 px below the table
  (`margin-top:-4px` on a 7 px handle) and the last column's ~1 px past it, so the layer adds 2 px of vertical and
  1 px of horizontal scroll to the grid box. True at 100% in the shipped app; at 50% it made `calzoom` W4's "the whole
  grid fits" (scrollHeight ≤ clientHeight) false, so W4 now measures the TABLE against the box. (2) A wide spanning
  cell's middle can sit on a column boundary, where the column handle takes the pointerdown, so `calzoom` W5 now
  clicks a point on the cell. Neither is a frozen-code change; the overhang could be clipped later (`.grid-resize-layer`
  is not frozen CSS, but clipping it would trim the last handles' grab area at 100% too, so it was left).
- **Handles scale with the grid** (7 px at 100%, under 2 px on screen at 25%). Not changed: their offsets (+4/-11,
  the 9 px clearance) are grid px in frozen code.
- **Proof so far:** new leg `tests/harness/t/zoomresize.js`, 40/40, wired into `gate.sh` after `calzoom` (red on a
  build of `main`: 50% column 194 vs 203, row 20 vs 36, cell edge unmoved, handles hidden); `calzoom` 24/24, `hdrfit`
  8/8 on colreuse and hdrmanualbraces, `sideboxes` 74/74, `npm run check` 40/40; by hand in the app's browser at 150%
  (real mouse drags: Date column 53 → 83 px for 45 px of pointer, a row to 40 px).
- ✅ **Full gate** (build `d80112ba…`, HARNESS_PORT 9683, 19:07–19:34, alone): **2,801 PASS, 0 FAIL, GATE PASSED**,
  `zoomresize` 40/40 and `calzoom` 24/24 among them, every baseline comparison identical (waterfall PDF, Excel parts,
  month PDF print path and writer).

### 🔨 22 Oct 2026: ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z AFTER EVERY KIND OF EDIT, AND A VIEW SWITCH IS NO UNDO STEP — branch `undo-keys` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/undo-keys`), from `main` at `55a9c61`; COMMITTED `3e904a4`, `main` (`83ee71d`) MERGED IN as `1e83cf0`; FULL GATE 2,770 / 2 on the pre-merge build `d007e3cf` (both `mvmenu`, expectations of the old rule, fixed and re-run 89/89 on the same build; the merged build's own gate was spoiled when the Mac slept); IN LOCAL `main` via `land-ready-1023` (combined gate 2,962 / 0, 23 Oct), NOT PUSHED

- **Asked:** *"Does command z and command shift z works for EVERYTHING?"* Investigated with real key presses in the
  pane, two gaps reported, and the owner said *"Yes, fix both"*. Mid-fix: *"also command z should not count switching
  between views as an undo step"*.
- **Gap 1, checkbox / switch / date box.** The keydown handler left ⌘Z to any focused `<input>` (so the browser's
  typing undo could run). Chrome leaves focus on a checkbox or switch after a click, and neither has an undo, so ⌘Z
  right after ticking a phase's Hiatus box or any Settings switch did NOTHING. ⭐ **A date box is the same, measured:**
  `document.execCommand('undo')` returns false in one after typing a date (true in text and number boxes). So ⌘Z after
  typing any phase start date did nothing either. Fix: `fieldOwnsUndoKeys()`: textarea, contenteditable, and inputs of
  type text/search/url/tel/email/password/number that are not read-only.
- **Gap 2, grid drag with a sidebar box focused.** The grid's drags `preventDefault()` their pointerdown (no text
  selection), which also stops the browser moving focus. Type a title, drag a column, ⌘Z: the keys went to the title
  and the drag stayed. Fix: a capture `pointerdown` on the document marks the focused input (`fieldUndoKeysStale`)
  when the press lands in `#table-wrap` or `.side-col` and the input is outside both. The app's undo then runs until
  the input gets `input`, a press inside it, or `focusin`. ⛔ **Marked, not blurred:** a blur fires the input's
  change event, which re-renders the grid mid-drag under frozen drag code holding the old DOM. ⚠️ Only an `<input>`
  is marked: a textarea / contenteditable is a note or header editor whose own buttons cancel the mousedown on
  purpose to keep the text focused.
- **View switch.** `viewMode` stays in `captureSnapshot()` (a file opens in its saved view, and a switch still dirties
  the file), but the undo history uses `undoStateJSON()` (the snapshot without `viewMode`) for the stack, the
  baseline and the compare, so a switch banks nothing and keeps the redo. `applySnapshotJSON()` writes the CURRENT
  view into the step before applying it, so undo/redo never change the view. Chosen here, the owner's to overturn:
  an undo of a month-only change made in the month view, pressed in the waterfall, changes nothing you can see.
- ⛔ **For the planned waterfall right-click menu** (`mockups/waterfall-context-menu.html`, ruled 22 Oct 2026, no code
  yet): its mockup says "Each item is one undo step", but **"Show this day or week in the month view" is a view switch
  and must bank NO undo step** under this rule, as the month menu's "Show this week in the waterfall" now banks none.
- **Not changed, for the owner:** `sidebarTab` is still in the history, so an undo can flip the sidebar between Show
  and Phases (the step's snapshot holds the tab it was banked on). Same class as the view switch; not asked.
- **No frozen code touched.** All three are in the undo section and the keydown handler of `src/legacy/app.js`.
- **Proof:** new leg **`undokeys`** (in-page, no state, `gate.sh` AFSPEC beside `dlgkeys`), 11/11: K1 Hiatus box, K2
  Single Column switch, K3 date box (each focused, ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z), K4 title + column drag, K5 click back in the title,
  K6 typing in it, K7 view switch, X1 text box keeps its keys, X2 a sidebar press does not take them, X3 the backup
  still records `viewMode`, E0. **Red on the unfixed engine at K1–K4 and K7**; mutants: old INPUT test → K1–K3, no
  stale mark → K4, `viewMode` kept in the history → K7. In the pane, real keys, on the dev build: the Hiatus box
  unticked/reticked, ⌘Z after a column drag put the width back with the title kept, ⌘Z in a date box cleared it,
  and undo/redo in the Month view stayed there with redo surviving two switches.
- ⚠️ **Traps for legs:** (1) `phaseedge` F9/F10 and `hiatusdrag` H2/H3/H10/H11 proved "nothing banked" by a Ctrl+Z
  landing on the view switch. They now check the Undo button is off and a Ctrl+Z changes nothing (after `load()`
  the stack is empty). Seven in-page legs' comments said a switch is an undo step; reworded to "until 22 Oct 2026",
  their logic left alone (still valid). (2) An undo restores the sidebar tab, which can hide the control a leg is
  about to focus (K1, K3 re-open Phases). (3) Headless Chrome drops focus from a date box once its date is set; the
  pane keeps it. (4) The pane's `computer` key cannot fire Chrome's native editing undo, so in-field ⌘Z cannot be
  observed there; `document.execCommand('undo')` is the proxy.
- ⭐ **The month view's "Show this week in the waterfall" is a view switch too, so it banks nothing now.** One Undo
  after it takes back the edit BEFORE it and stays in the waterfall. `mvmenu` K2u/I7u asserted the old rule (one undo
  goes back to the month view) and were the gate's only two failures; they now assert the new one (undo goes past the
  item, stays in the waterfall, redo puts the earlier edit back). The other three items are still one step each.
- `npm run check` 40/40, build `d007e3cf…`.
- **Full gate on build `d007e3cf…` (port 8712, 22 Oct 19:34:17–20:01:13, alone; it waited for the resize-zoom
  session's gate): 2,770 PASS, 2 FAIL** (`mvmenu` K2u, I7u: above). Every frozen output identical to baseline:
  waterfall PDF, Excel parts, the month print documents, 0 clipped, 76 ids. `undokeys` 11/11, `phaseedge` 23/23,
  `hiatusdrag` 13/13, `dlgkeys` 16/16, `filekeys` 16/16, `labelsetup` 41/41. **`mvmenu` re-run on the same build after
  its fix: 89/89** (another session's gate was running by then; it passed anyway).

### 🔧 22 Oct 2026 (late): `sideboxes` R16 WAS A FLAKY LEG, NOT A REGRESSION — FIXED IN THE LEG; NO APP CHANGE — branch `claude/hungry-chandrasekhar-519cf8` (worktree), from `main` at `83ee71d`; IN LOCAL `main` via `land-ready-1023` (combined gate 2,962 / 0, 23 Oct), NOT PUSHED

- **Reported:** R16 (*"a box carried to the bottom of its column scrolls that column, not the grid"*) failed in
  another session's full gate, `{"left":0,"grid":120,"gridWas":120}`, and 77/78 alone four times on `main`'s build.
  The `sidebox-own-scroll` session had gated the same leg 78/78 that afternoon.
- **Same bytes, different result.** `main`'s build here is sha256 `57056dcb…`, byte-identical to the build that
  passed 78/78 and to the live file. `src/` has not changed since `f77c353`. Alone it failed 3 of 3 runs.
- **Cause, measured with a probe leg (since deleted):** under `--virtual-time-budget`, `requestAnimationFrame`
  never fires. That was 0 frames in a bare 400 ms sleep, and 0 with this drag held at the edge for 5 s while waiting
  for the scroll (the drag asked for 1–2 frames and got none). So "wait for the thing" alone cannot fix it: the thing
  never happens. With frames fed from `setTimeout(16)` the app's own tick scrolled the column 60 px in 100 ms, and
  the grid did not move. The 400 ms sleep passed only when a stray frame landed, which the afternoon's run happened
  to get.
- **The app is fine, checked in the in-app browser** (`?state=sideboxes`, 1024 × 768, `main`'s build):
  - With a real mouse, a drag from the left box's grip to the column's bottom scrolled the column 10 px in the
    21 ms between press and release, and the grid stayed at 0.
  - Held at the bottom edge, the column scrolled to its end (703 px = scroll height − view) over 81 frames. The grid
    stayed at 0 and the drop outline stayed up.
  - Escape left the box where it was, cleared the outline, and made no undo step.
  - ⚠️ **The pane was HIDDEN** (this session was not open in a window), so the page ran as a background page and
    made frames only now and then: one held run got 1 frame in 400 ms. A screenshot forces a frame, so the
    held-drag proof was taken by screenshotting while the drag was held. Don't read frame timing from a hidden pane.
- **The fix, in `tests/harness/t/sideboxes.js` only:** for R16's one drag, `window.requestAnimationFrame` /
  `cancelAnimationFrame` are swapped for virtual-time timer versions, and the leg waits (`T.until`, 30 × 50 ms) for
  the column to scroll instead of sleeping 400 ms. They are put back in a `finally` only AFTER Escape, so the drag's
  `end()` cancels a pending timer frame. With the native cancel, that frame would run the tick on a finished drag
  (`sideDrag` is null there) and throw. `out.r16` now also records `frames`.
- **Proof:**
  - Old R16 on `main`'s build: 77/78, 3 of 3 runs.
  - New leg on the same build: 78/78, 5 runs in a row, identical numbers each time (`{"left":30,"grid":120,"gridWas":120,"frames":4}`).
  - Mutant M1 (the tick always scrolls the grid, the pre-`sidebox-own-scroll` behaviour): red at R16 alone, column
    0, grid 120 → 390.
  - Mutant M2 (no auto-scroll): red at R16 alone, nothing moved.
  - F13 (no console errors) stayed green in all runs, so the timer frames disturb nothing else.
  - Full gate NOT run: one leg file changed, no `src/`. Other `sideboxes` checks and the gate's count of 78 are
    unchanged.
- ⭐ **Learned, for any leg testing rAF-driven behaviour** (drag auto-scroll, Mantine transitions, anything on a
  frame loop): waiting is not enough, because frames never come. Supply them for the duration of the gesture,
  restore them after it ends, and keep the assertion about WHAT moved, not how far. A leg that passes on rAF is
  passing on luck.
- ⏭ **Next:** commit on this branch is the owner's call (picker). Nothing to push: the app did not change.

### 📝 22 Oct 2026: THE EXCEL-GAPS LIST, AND A MOCKUP OF A WATERFALL RIGHT-CLICK MENU ([`mockups/waterfall-context-menu.html`](mockups/waterfall-context-menu.html)); ITS THREE QUESTIONS RULED; NO CODE

- **The request.** The owner asked what Excel-like functionality SPTCal still lacks (*"this app needs to replace
  excel but purely in the use case of creating this calendars"*). The answer, in chat: formatting is mostly covered,
  and the gaps are Excel's EDITING habits (waterfall date drags, arrow keys / Delete, copy and paste, find and replace,
  a right-click menu, fill on many cells) and its "duplicate the tab" habits (revision marking, scenarios, private
  comments, paste into an email). From that list the owner picked:
  - **Item 2 (arrow keys, Tab, Delete) and item 3 (copy / cut / paste notes, drag a note to another week):** given to
    their own session, "Waterfall arrow keys, Delete, and copy/paste notes" (worktree). It asks its rulings by picker
    before building. Tracker cards `waterfall-keyboard` and `waterfall-copy-paste-notes`.
  - **Item 5, a waterfall right-click menu: a MOCKUP only** (this entry). Tracker card `waterfall-right-click-menu`.
  - **Item 8, scenarios:** a full workup, written by the session "Scenarios plan: compare calendar options" in this
    checkout (`SCENARIOS-PLAN.md`; it adds its own entry). Tracker card `scenarios`.
- ⚠️ **A correction to that list.** It proposed "freeze panes" as a frozen edit. The heading row is ALREADY frozen
  on screen (`.sheet-table th` is `position:sticky`) and repeats on every printed page. Only the exported workbook
  has no frozen panes (`exportExcel`'s `views` carries no split), and a sticky date column makes no sense with year
  blocks side by side.
- **The mockup** is built the way `mockups/month-context-menu.html` was, and reuses that mockup's menu engine. The
  waterfall is captured from the build (`dist/` of 21 Oct, `halfdays.sptcal`, synthetic) with headless Chrome
  `--dump-dom`. It has `#table-wrap`'s markup verbatim minus the resize handles, and only the CSS rules that match
  it. Open it through the harness server at `/mockups/waterfall-context-menu.html`.
  - **Its shape:** the same look, keys and fixed order as the month day menu. A clipboard row first (Cut · Copy ·
    Paste · Clear, as Excel's Paste Options row), then "This week" (Add / Edit this week's note · Add 1-week phase
    here · Add hiatus ▸), then one section for the cell clicked: a phase week (Start / End <phase> this week, Expand
    cells, Swap column left / right, Colour…, Show in Phases card), a note (Edit, colour, back to automatic text), a
    hiatus band, a holiday in that week. Then Size (reset row height / column width), and Show this week in the
    month view. A column heading gets a short menu of its own.
  - **Right-click inside a highlight acts on all of it**; outside it, the cell is highlighted first.
  - Refused items are greyed with their reason (the standing rule). The clipboard items only CALL the item 2/3
    session's actions.
- ✅ **Ruled (owner, 22 Oct 2026, in chat):** *"keep the clipboard row at the top. Clear can remove an automatic
  note, and keep the Size section."* The mockup's side panel now records them.
  1. **The clipboard row stays at the top**, as Excel's Paste Options row.
  2. **Clear can remove an automatic note.** It is stored as cleared (`userNotes[week] = {text:''}`), stays empty
     when dates move, and Reset Notes brings it back. This is the first Delete note in either menu; the month menu has
     none (ruling Q5). The ruling was passed to the item 2/3 session, since its Delete key does the same thing.
  3. **The Size section stays** (reset row height / column width).
  4. **Show this DAY or week in the month view** (owner, 22 Oct 2026: *"should have show this day or week in the
     month view button"*; by picker, "Day when it knows the day"). The mockup already ended with "Show this week",
     which was easy to miss. Now:
     - **on a dated note** it reads "Show this day in the month view" and lands on that day, highlighted for a
       moment. The day comes from the note line's trailing date, by the rule `notesForWaterfallDate` already uses
       to put a waterfall line on its day; only a date inside the note's own week counts. The mockup shows it on
       the wrap note (Tue 1 Sep);
     - **on a holiday shown in a Notes cell**, the same, on the holiday's day;
     - **everywhere else** it stays "Show this week", and the week's row is highlighted.
     The highlight must be drawn OVER the month view, never written into it: the month view is frozen.
- ⚠️ **The menu is tall** (about 17 lines on a phase week), taller than a short window. The mockup caps it at the
  window height and lets it scroll. A scroll inside the menu must not trip the close-on-scroll listener, so that
  listener skips scrolls whose target is inside the menu. The build needs both.
- ⏭ **Next:** a plan and a build. Wait for the item 2/3 work first: the clipboard row only calls its Cut / Copy /
  Paste / Clear actions. ✅ **Planned and built 23 Oct 2026, CUT DOWN by the owner first** (the entry at the top,
  "THE WATERFALL RIGHT-CLICK MENU, CUT DOWN…"): the mockup's long menu below is NOT what was built.
- **Needs no frozen edit**, on the month menu's reasoning (MONTH-CONTEXT-MENU-PLAN §4): it reads `#table-wrap`,
  opens a body-level menu, and calls existing writers (`setPhaseStart`, `phaseEdgePlan` → `phaseEdgeWrite`,
  `batchFill`, `doSwapMove`, `openPhaseColorPop`, `addHiatusRow`, `addPhaseHere`, `openNoteCellEditor`).
- ⚠️ **A trap found while testing it:** the browser pane's `zoom` screenshot fires a `resize`, so a page that closes
  its menu on resize shows no menu in a zoomed capture. Use a plain screenshot.

  (Cut · Copy · Paste · Clear) will call `wfNotesCut` / `wfNotesCopy` / `wfNotesPaste` / `wfNotesClear`.

- **Asked** (relayed by the session that started this one, the owner's picks from its "Excel features SPTCal lacks"
  list): item 2, *"arrow keys, Tab and Delete on the selection"*, and item 3, *"copy, cut and paste notes, and drag a
  note to another week"*. **Ruled by picker, 22 Oct 2026, each the recommended answer** (plan §6): Q1 Delete empties
  the Notes cells, automatic notes too, nothing else touched; Q2 a copied note carries its look; Q3 a paste replaces;
  Q4 a drag onto a note asks before replacing. Tracker cards `waterfall-keyboard`, `waterfall-copy-paste-notes`.
- **Where:** `src/legacy/app.js` "EXCEL'S EDITING KEYS", right after the text style bar's ⌘A handler. Small hooks in
  that bar's own code (not frozen): `textSelFocus` / `navCursor` / `wfNoteDrag`, the pointerdown gesture (a press on
  a HIGHLIGHTED note with text becomes a drag at the sweep's own thresholds; every gesture records its far corner),
  `redrawTextSel` (draws the drop outlines and the one message via `drawWfNoteOverlay`), and the Enter/typing
  handler's guard (Escape during a drag is the drag's). `openNoteEditor` now reads `sheetAutoNotesFor()`, the same
  lookup, factored out so the keys and the editor cannot disagree about a week's automatic note. Help paragraph
  "Moving around, and moving notes" in `src/index.html`; two classes in legacy.css (`.text-sel-drop`,
  `.text-sel-chip`).
- **How it works.** `textSelMatrix()` reads the frozen table's own shape (rowSpan/colSpan) into slots; arrows step
  over blank gaps (they can hold no highlight) and stop on every keyed cell, empty Notes cells and bands included
  (`navCursor` keeps the column across a wide cell). Delete / cut / paste / drop write `userNotes`, `noteColors`,
  `noteFontSize`, `textStyle.note` through `saveNoteEdit()` and call frozen `render()`; `wfNoteStep()` makes each ONE
  undo step (flush, change, render, bank now, the month note drag's pattern). Copy is Excel's text form (every line
  ends in `\n`, a multi-line note quoted) plus SPTCal's own clipboard type `application/x-sptcal-notes` carrying
  the look; `wfNoteClip` remembers the last copy so a paste of the same text brings the look back even without that
  type. Paste goes down the Notes cells on screen (`wfNoteSeq()`), so hiatus rows are stepped over; clipboard data
  is held to a saved file's rules (`wfCleanClipItem`, `stripXmlIllegal`).
- ⭐ **For the right-click menu** (the parent session's mockup): `wfNotesCopy()`, `wfNotesCut()`,
  `wfNotesPaste(text, rich)`, `wfNotesClear()` are the whole job, and the listeners only call them. A menu's Paste has
  no clipboard event, so it would read `navigator.clipboard.readText()` (a permission prompt in Chrome) and pass the
  text; `wfNoteClip` brings the look back.
- **No frozen code, no setting, no snapshot key, no id, no version bump.** Stays out of `undo-keys`' way: neither the
  ⌘Z handler nor the undo section is touched (only `pushUndoSnapshot()` / `markDirty()` are called), so the two
  branches should merge clean.
- **Proof:** `wfkeys` leg 84/84 (new fixture `wfkeys.sptcal`), **red on the pre-change build (52 of 84)** and on four
  mutants (an automatic note deleted instead of cleared → D4, G17; paste never carrying the look → C4, C6, C14, C15,
  C18; the drag never asking → G7, G8; no column memory → K15, K18–K21). `wfkeysreal.mjs` 6/6 with TRUSTED CDP input,
  0 of 6 on the pre-change build. Both wired into `gate.sh` after `textstyle`. `npm run check` 40/40,
  `check-refs.py` CLEAN. In the app pane (port 9620, real clicks and keys): arrows and Shift+arrows, a real drag moving
  "Table read" with its look, the question on a drop onto "Blocker" (Cancel kept both), Delete, ⌘Z twice back to an
  empty undo stack.
- ⚠️ **Learned, measured:**
  - **Chrome runs a key binding's Copy / Cut / Paste even while they are disabled**, so the clipboard events fire
    with nothing selected and nothing editable: `wfkeysreal` passes with the app's `beforecopy` / `beforecut` /
    `beforepaste` listeners REMOVED. They stay, as insurance for the native Edit menu, which no headless leg can
    reach. (This session first wrote the opposite into a comment; the mutant corrected it.)
  - **The heading row's drawn bottom is a `thead th`'s, never the `<thead>`'s**: the sticky rule is on the cells, so
    a scrolled pane's `<thead>` rect says the heading takes no room (rows were parked under it; `sheetHeadBottom()`).
  - **In a leg, `scrollIntoView({block:'nearest'})` parks a row UNDER the sticky heading**, and the click lands on the
    heading. Centre it.
  - **A plain click never makes a phase selection** (`gridSel`): only a sweep, ⌘-click or Shift-click does.
  - **The pane's `computer` keys cannot fire Chrome's own Copy / Paste**, as they could not fire native undo
    (`undo-keys`). Only CDP's `commands` can, so a physical ⌘C in the installed app window is NOT verified.
- ⚠️ **Possible side effect:** this session first ran its legs on HARNESS_PORT 9711, which is `sidebox-own-scroll`'s
  preview port in the shared launch list, and `run.sh` stops any `srv.js` on its port. Nothing was listening there
  afterwards; whether that preview had been running before is unknown. The legs moved to 9623. This session's preview
  is `wf-keys-9620`, added to the main checkout's `.claude/launch.json`.
- **Known limit, not changed:** the keys work with focus on the page itself (the gridSel keyboard rule), so after a
  click on a toolbar button (Undo, say), click a cell first. The style bar's buttons keep focus on the page.
- ⏭ **Next:** merging into `main` and pushing are the owner's, each asked separately; the owner held both (22 Oct),
  perhaps to land with `undo-keys`. `sideboxes` R16 wants its own look (above; proposed as its own session).

### 🔨 22 Oct 2026: THE TEXT STYLE HIGHLIGHT STAYS ON ITS CELLS WHEN YOU ZOOM — branch `textsel-zoom` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/textsel-zoom`), from `main` at `4b95581`; COMMITTED on the branch on the owner's word (picker, 22 Oct: *"Commit on the branch"*, push *"Not yet"*); IN LOCAL `main` via `land-ready-1023` (23 Oct, combined gate 2,962 / 0), NOT pushed

  pass at 50/150/200% on `colreuse`): only two things break at a zoom other than 100%. (1) The resize handles,
  hidden while zoomed, fixed in their own session (`resize-zoom`, frozen edit owner-approved by picker, 22 Oct).
  (2) The text style highlight, fixed here. Checked and fine: the phase selection and its outline, Swap Column, side
  boxes, month bar move / edge / reorder drags (a two-day drag moved two days at 50% and at 200%), hiatus bands, the
  day menu, the cell editors (pressing the zoom control closes them, so none is stranded), the grid fit, both print
  paths (they lay out off screen in `#print-root`, unzoomed) and the writers (no DOM). Minor, not fixed: the month
  grips keep their on-screen size, so at 25–30% a bar under 46 screen px gets no reorder grip.
- ⚠️ **What was learned: rows lay out at a different height in the grid's OWN px at each zoom** — 20.0 at 100%,
  20.8 at 50%, 20.1 at 200% (measured in a 1600×1000 window on the `colreuse` leg). So any box drawn inside `#table-wrap` in grid px at
  one zoom drifts at another, ~0.4–0.8 px per row. A text highlight 45 rows down sat 19 px off at 50% and 76 px off
  at 200%, until the settle timer's render at 100% redrew it. **Anything drawn in grid px must be redrawn after
  every zoom change** — this is what the resize handles will need too (sent to that session, 22 Oct).
- **Built** (no frozen code): `setCalZoom()` now redraws the text highlight beside the phase selection it already
  redrew: `if(v === 'sheet'){ redrawGridOverlay(null); if(textSel.size) redrawTextSel(null); }`.
- **Proof:** new `calzoom` W8 (press a date cell 45 rows down at 100%, zoom to 50% and 200%, highlight within 1 px of
  the cell), red on the pre-change build (`z200: [0, 75.9, 0, 1.7]`), green after; `calzoom` 25/25, `textstyle`
  51/51, `npm run check` 40/40, build `d4580885…`. In the browser: 0 px off at 50/150/200%, selected at 100% or 50%.
  Full gate not run: the highlight is session UI, never in a cell, an export or a saved file.
- ⚠️ **Will meet `resize-zoom`** in `setCalZoom()`: that branch edits the settle timer right under this line, so
  expect a small textual conflict when both land. Keep both.
- ⏭ **Next:** land it when the owner says (they chose *"Not yet"*; one option is together with `resize-zoom`, which edits
  the same function). Merging into `main` and the push are each a separate ask.

### ✅ 22 Oct 2026 (LIVE at `f77c353`, verified byte-identical, `57056dcb`; no version cut): THE SIDE BOXES AND THE WATERFALL SCROLL SEPARATELY — branch `sidebox-own-scroll` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/sidebox-own-scroll`), from `main` at `55a9c61`; COMMITTED on the owner's word, and a fast-forward into local `main` plus ONE push approved by picker (22 Oct): *"Commit on the branch"*, *"Merge into local main"*, *"Push and verify live"*

  boxes live up and down. instead it should only scroll the viewport or only scroll the sidebar with the boxes
  dependent on where your cursor is"*. Not ruled further.

  that session). `syncSideScroll()` and its capturing `scroll` listener are GONE: do not put a scroll link back.
  The wheel scrolls whatever is under the pointer, so a colour key stays in view while the grid scrolls.
- **What did not change:** a box is still drawn level with its sheet row in its own column (`sideRowMap`), so the
  two line up whenever scrolled alike; a drop reads the row off the column as drawn. Writers, layout, save format:
  untouched. No frozen code.
- **Two things made to fit:** (1) a column that has only just appeared opens level with the grid
  (`sideLevelWithGrid`, called from `renderSideBoxes` when there was no old stack), so a box dropped on an empty
  side shows where it was let go; (2) the drag's auto-scroll (`beginSideDrag`'s `tick`) scrolls the column the box
  is over, and the grid only over an empty side.
- **Proof (build from this branch):** `sideboxes` 78/78 (R6/R7 rewritten, R15–R18 new), **red on the pre-change
  build** at R6, R7, R16; `sbnoexport` 28/28; `calzoom` 24/24; `textstyle` 51/51; `npm run check` 40/40. In the

  nothing in `#table-wrap`, the writers or the save path (memory: chrome changes get targeted legs).
- ⚠️ **Headless trap, new:** headless Chrome runs few animation frames in a leg, so a rAF-driven auto-scroll moves

  ⚠️ **CORRECTED the same evening (the entry at the top):** "few" was ZERO on an idle machine. The 78/78 here got
